Grader Interface Contract\n\nYou **must** implement exactly this. An automated Playwright script checks\nagainst it — this is a hard requirement, not a suggestion.\n\n- A canvas element `<canvas id="raptor-canvas">` whose internal pixel buffer is\n  exactly 320x200 (`canvas.width === 320`, `canvas.height === 200`), independent\n  of any CSS display scaling.\n- Controls: `ArrowUp` / `ArrowDown` / `ArrowLeft` / `ArrowRight` move the ship;\n  the Space bar fires. The\n  grader polls it, drives real keyboard input to test M2/M3/M4/M5, and takes a\n  canvas screenshot to test M1. Yes, this is consistent: `PALETTE_DAT 0x00010000` = FILE0001.GLB item 0, and `ATENTION_TXT 0x00000000` = FILE0000.GLB item 0.\n\nKey details:\n- Decryption key: `serial = "32768GLB"`\n- `_SCOTTGAME` is defined, so all keyfile entries are decrypted with GLB_DeCrypt(serial, ...)\n- SEED — need to find its value. Let\'s search.\n\nGLB_DeCrypt:\n```c\nINT klen = strlen(key);\nINT prev_byte;\nINT kidx;\nCHAR dchr;\nkidx = SEED % klen;\nprev_byte = key[kidx];\nwhile (length--)\n{\n   dchr = (*buffer - key[kidx] - prev_byte) % 256;\n   prev_byte = *buffer;\n   *buffer++ = dchr;\n   if (++kidx >= klen) kidx = 0;\n}\n```\n\nNote: in C, for byte values (promoted to int, 0-255), the `% 256` of a negative value... In the C language, `dchr = (*buffer - key[kidx] - prev_byte) % 256;` — if the value is negative, C\'s % preserves the sign of the dividend, so dchr could be negative, and then it\'s stored in CHAR (implementation-defined, but usually two\'s complement). Then prev_byte = *buffer (the original byte value, 0-255).\n\nActually, in two\'s complement, the result of (a - b - c) % 256 when negative equals the equivalent value in two\'s complement of (a - b - c) mod 256. So in practice: dchr = (byte - key - prev) mod 256, computed as unsigned.\n\nTherefore decryption is: out[i] = (in[i] - key[kidx] - prev) mod 256; prev = in[i]; kidx advances cyclically, starting from SEED % 8.\n\nThe SEED value is needed.
